thing to other gases does not why is it that some things react violently while do not it turns out that we have a pretty good way to predict what reactions will occur under what conditions some reactions require energy While others give off energy in chemistry these are called endothermic and exothermic reactions respectively in this video I'm going to show you how the chemistry behind these are broken and new ones are formed breaking bonds generally requires energy while forming new bonds generally releases energy but the balance of of energy between these two processes determines whether a reaction is exothermic or endothermic so for example a methane molecule CH4 which by the way is what natural gas is mostly made up of has four carbon hydrogen bonds and it consumes some energy to break these into a free Carbon and four free hydrogens then we get energy back when these free carbons and hydrogens bond with oxygen to form H2O and CO2 in other words the resulting you're interested in getting more in depth now don't let the formula intimidate you it's essentially an equation describing how the energy of a Quantum system evolves over time the total energy on the right is equal to the kinetic energy plus potential energy on the left we'll use the simplest atom for illustration the hydrogen atom the Sher equation shows that its electron exists in regions around the nule called orbitals these are represented by a cloud because the electron does not have a specific location it has a probability of being somewhere within the cloud when two hydrogen atoms interact to form a molecule their electron clouds overlap leading to new Quantum States for the electron these states are lower in energy making the combined structure of two hydrogen atoms more stable than the atoms on their own this charge shows how the energy of the system is reduced when they combine to form a molecule the excess energy is released because the product of this reaction a hydrogen molecule is at a lower energy level than the reactants two individual hydrogen atoms it's an exothermic reaction this is by the way why hydrogen in nature is generally found as the more stable H2 molecule rather than the less stable individual atoms the energy state of a hydrogen at at versus a hydrogen molecule can be calculated by solving the shinger equation for the two structures this concept can be applied in principle to any other atoms that combine to form molecules if the resulting molecule is in a lower energy state it releases energy in an exothermic reaction conversely if the resulting molecule is in a higher energy state it requires energy and is an endothermic reaction the more energetically stable a molecule is the less potential energy it has in other words it stores less energy conversely when molecules are less energetically stable they have higher potential energy and thus store more energy CO2 and H2O are more energetically stable than glucose and methane that's why glucose and methane store more energy than carbon dioxide and water now let's go one level deeper and examine why it is that certain electron configurations have more or less energy than others there's one key principle that helps explain not only this but also why different electron configurations exist in the first place that principle is the poly Exclusion Principle which states that no two Fons electrons being firmance can have identical Quantum states to put it more simply it says that no two identical electrons can be at the same place at the same time now it so happens that all electrons are identical to to each other except for their property of quantum spin they can have either an up or down spin in the context of the poly Exclusion Principle this means that we can have up to two electrons in a given suborbital because only an UPS Spin and down spin electron can be at the same place at the same time and no more this is important to understand because it means that after a suborbital is filled with two electrons any additional electrons have to be in a different location within the atom this leads to the arrangement of electrons in different orbitals which are at different energy levels now let's think about how important this principle of nature is if all electrons could be at the same place at the same time there would be no differences in chemical bonds and no diversity in chemical behavior or energy optimization atoms would pretty much not react with each other now spin doesn't affect the energy levels of electrons which is why the Schrodinger equation doesn't include it however this spin property allows two electrons to occupy the same energy State all other electrons must occupy different orbitals with different energy levels these different energy levels provide opportunities to optimize energies when two atoms combine a chemical reaction between two or more atoms is essentially about arranging the available electron of the participating atoms in the most energyefficient way in some cases combining different atoms creates new opportunities for electron Arrangements that are more energetically favorable and so they release excess energy in the newly formed molecular system this is what drives exothermic reactions now the question you might have is what determines whether certain electron configurations are more energy efficient than others we can get a good idea of what the most stable electron configurations are by looking at the noble elements on the periodic table the shinger equation shows that these are the most stable electron configurations I have a video explaining this further if you want to know more details about it on your top right over here these elements have a full complement of electrons in their outermost or valence shell just about every bond that forms tine atoms to make a molecule is an attempt by the shared electrons to achieve a full complimentary shell like one of the noble elements so for example when two hydrogen atoms share their electrons with an oxygen atom it is an attempt by the system to form a stable electron structure of the two Noble elements helium and neon the hydrogen atoms by borrowing an extra electron mimics the stable electron configuration of helium and the oxygen atom by borrowing two extra electrons attempts to mimic the stable electron configuration of neon similarly nearly all the atoms that form molecules in nature attempt to mimic the more stable electron configurations of one of the noble elements exothermic reactions are those that release energy into the surroundings we can understand this using the principle of conservation of energy energy is never created or destroyed only transferred an exothermic reaction the potential energy stor stor in the chemical bonds of the reactants is greater than in that stored in the chemical bonds of the products the difference can be released as heat light other forms of energy such as sound now what about the opposite endothermic reactions are those that absorb energy from their surroundings a familiar example is the chemical reaction that happens in an instant cold pack such a pack typically contains ammonium nitrate and water in separate sealed pouches in the pack when the pouch is broken the ammonium nitrate dissolves in the water causing an endothermic reaction that absorbs heat and cools the pack down in endothermic reactions like this the chemical bonds in the reactants are stronger and breaking them requires more energy than is released when the products are formed the energy required to break the bonds of the reactants comes from the water and the surroundings the surroundings lose heat and that heat energy gets absorbed by the reaction as a result the temperature drops and it feels cold unlike the previous example of combustion here the system gains overall energy now since the products are going to a higher energy State you might ask wait a minute overall energy is not being lowered so how can this reaction occur in the first place well this is where the second law of Thermodynamics links up with quantum mechanics while minimizing energy is a key concept of physics we also have to consider entropy of the system as a whole nature tends to favor processes that increase entropy in the universe or its oversimplified description increased disorder and scattering even if a reaction absorbs energy if it increases overall entropy it can still drive a reaction so while breaking the bonds between the ammonium and nitrate takes more energy than is released when the ammonium and nitrate ions dissolve in water it is more than made up for by the increase in entropy or disorder that results from the breakdown of the crystal lattice of the ammonium nitrate to sum it up exothermic reactions release energy making the surroundings warmer while endothermic reactions absorb energy cooling their surroundings the driving force behind both these reactions is the balance between the energy state of the electrons in the molecules and entropy so this requires a more nuanced understanding Beyond Simple Quantum models like the shener equation for a hydrogen atom understanding the physics Behind These reactions shows us that chemical reactions aren't just about molecules colliding they're about energy transfer obeying the laws of quantum mechanics and thermodynamics so if it's the crackle of a fire or the chill of an ice pack it's all about the flow of energy which is ultimately all about physics see you in the next white any mirror-like metallic structure we see is due to the metal surface features a smoother polished surface can result in a shinier color now some metals such as gold have a different color or not this perception has been shaped over many decades from highly publicized nuclear accidents the most recent of which are Chernobyl in 1986 and Fukushima in 2011 but there's a different type of nuclear technology using molten salt and thorium which can eliminate many of the problems that commercial nuclear power plants of today are riddle with to understand how molten salt nuclear reactors can be so much better than current reactor designs let's compare how traditional nuclear power plants work and contrast that with a molten salt reactor design in the core of a traditional reactor where the energy is created solid nuclear fuel is placed in a series of rods the fishing reactions take place here the fuel traditionally is uranium 238 with a tiny amount of uranium 235 only about 3 to 4% this is the fle part it's important to understand that u238 which makes up about 96 to 97% of the material in the rods is not fishable only the uranium 235 can be split in a fishion reaction to make energy so only a small fraction of the fuel gets used in the process this leads to one of the problems with this type of nuclear fuel even though we can get a lot of energy from this tiny fraction it is still quite wasteful but the bigger problem with so much u238 is that although it does not split it transmutes to highly Radioactive isot such as plutonium 239 this is a highly toxic waste product that needs to be stored for thousands of years before it decays to safer and more stable elements this is the core of the nuclear waste problem which many in the general public object to the speed of the reaction that is the rate at which energy is produced in the reactor is regulated by control rods these control rods contain Neutron absorbing material such as cadmium or Boron which reduce the number of neutrons available to split the u235 the control rods can control or stop the reaction if we didn't have this the fuel rods could get overheat it and cause a meltdown you should also understand that when the u235 splits the neutrons produced in this reaction are usually too fast and quickly dissipate this makes them unavailable for the fishion process so a moderator is used to slow down the neutrons so that they are more likely to cause fishing in the fuel these moderators are typically water heavy water or graphite heavy water is where the hydrogen atoms in the water molecule are replaced by detarium that have one extra neutrons in their nuclei and that brings us to the last Central component of the reactor the cooling this is done by transferring or harvesting the heat produced in the fishing process without this process the heat produced in the reactor would have nowhere to go this could risk a meltdown failure of this aspect is what happened in highly publicized nuclear accidents at Chernobyl Fukushima and 3M Island traditional nuclear reactors typically use the same moderating regular water or heavy water to transfer the heat away from the core using regular water is cheap and simple heavy water is more expensive but it absorbs fewer neutrons keeping more of them available for the fishing process so that's why it's used if using ordinary water is the coolant it can be either directly connected or connected via secondary Loop to a generator to make electricity the water is then cooled down and reused as the coolant in the reactor similar to the way the coolant in your car is reused after being cooled in the radiator this is how most commercial reactors work roughly speaking there are lots of different designs and there are some differences in features but what I discussed so far applies to most nuclear reactors in use today now let's contrast this with how molten salt reactors work keep in mind that as of gener 2024 these types of reactors are in various stages of development and have not been commercialized yet there are two main differences first instead of solid fuels the fuel is dissolved in molten salt which is in liquid form the second difference is that instead of water as the coolant they use a second molten salt as the coolant these have several advantages as well as some disadvantages first these salts are not table salts like sodium chloride you have at home but more complex salts made in Labs there's no standard formula for the salt and each producer may have their own specification the type of salts used by Copenhagen atomics one of the Premier molten salt reactor research and Manufacturing facilities which I had the pleasure of visiting recently Ed for example fluoride lithium based salts the main requirement for the salt is that it must contain some fishable fuel dissolved in it which can drive the nuclear process this visionable material can be the same u235 mixture used in most traditional power plants but it can also be something like thorium or plutonium the latter could come from nuclear waste produced by traditional reactors uranium and thorium are mined and there is enough on Earth to keep reactors running for hundreds of years the big picture I want to paint for you is that in such reactors the ficial material such as uranium is now part of the salt which is in liquid form this can allow it to move and circulate this is very different from traditional designs where the uranium is a solid and stays stagnant in the control rods it just sits in the core and can't go anywhere why is it better to have a design where the nuclear fuel can circulate there's several reasons related to both operational efficiency and safety first because it's molten it operates at much higher temperatures and can carry a lot more heat to give you an idea the maximum temperature of the molten salt in the reactor is about 700° C whereas the operating temperature of water under very high pressure is only about 300° C the molten salt does not operate under high pressure which is another good safety feature because there's no chance of pressure vessel failure furthermore because the fuel salt is being pumped into the reactor to keep it circulating if anything goes wrong for example if the pump stops working the fuel will simply drain out via gravity into the bottom holding tanks this would happen without any human intervention and is a big safety feature this is almost like an onoff switch which essentially just turns the reactor off in case of failure the heat from the reaction cannot overwhelm the cooling apparatus molten salt also happens to have a chemical property which makes it safer when molten salt becomes too hot it naturally expands that is the distance between its molecules increases this reduces the nuclear chain reactions because the distance between the fil atoms increases so the likelihood of neutron nuclei collisions decreases and the Chain Reaction slows down all by itself if the salt gets too hot this is like a built-in thermodynamic safety valve so in case of trouble the salt can be just allowed to overheat and the chain reactions will be reduced by itself this makes the idea of runaway overheating or meltdown virtually obsolete now there is an additional major operation AAL benefit in conventional reactors the fuel is solid when the fuel runs out they have to be shut down so that the fuel rods can be replaced this can be costly but in molten Sal reactor since the fuel is circulating the reactor does not have to be shut down to be refueled it can be refueled on the go that is while it is still in operation and by the same method scrubbers can be installed which can take undesirable fision byproducts like xenon gas out of the fuel while it's still running finally because the core bers virtue of using molten salt is very hot 500 plus de C it has a much better thermal efficiency you can thus make more heat from a smaller core so the reactor is not only unpressurized but it also has a smaller footprint so it costs less to build for a given energy output the reactor designed by Copenhagen atomics are so efficient and small in fact that the whole thing can fit in a 40t container and produced on an assembly line drastically reducing manufacturing costs so if molten salt reactors are so great why are they not everywhere this is a good question and the answer is fairly simple molten salts are traditionally highly corrosive corrosion is what we commonly know as rust on iron but more things than just iron can rust and that in general is what corrosion is chemically it's when the metal container housing the molten salt reacts with oxygen if you want to see corrosion in action just put a piece of iron and salt water and see how it begins to rust a similar problem can occur with molten salt reactors if the corrosion problem is not addressed Leakes could occur so anything touching the salt must either be replaced very often or be made of high temperature non-corrosive materials which are very expensive the way Copenhagen atomics has addressed this problem is by keeping the salt Ultra Clean that is they have found a way to eliminate essentially all the moisture and air from the process so that it does not corrode salt cannot corrode anything if there's no moisture or oxygen available another drawback with molten salt reactors is that they have not been commercially proven this is something that research and manufacturing companies like Copenhagen atomics are working very hard to do but before they're proven there's always going to be some doubt about their feasibility now I want to address perhaps the biggest reason for the Public's objection to nuclear reactors the nuclear waste issue molten salt reactors would not help much to solve the issue of significant radioactive waste if we use the same uranium fuel source as today's conventional reactors that is the issue of what to do with the radioactive plutonium 239 would still be a problem however if we use thorium instead of uranium in these reactors there would be a lot less radioactive waste and the severity of the radioactivity would be much more manageable thorian fishing byproducts have much lower half-lies on the order of several decades instead of thousands of years for Uranium 235 before they can be safely handled thorium produces not only a lot less radioactive waste but it's also much more abundant than uranium if you want to know more about Thorium I have a dedicated video about it right here in a nutshell the reason it does not produce as much radioactivity is because there's a lot less plutonium 239 produced in the fishing process when thorium is split versus uranium 235 as far as safety is concerned the worst case scenario for molten salid reactors would probably be if the molten salt with the radioactive material leaked somehow while this would release some radioactivity the salt would become solid as it cools and contain the radioactive fuel so it would be possible to clean it up pretty effectively and thanks to physics and the built-in safety features of molten salt reactors we would never see disaster such as Chernobyl or Fukushima again because they can physically not happen the bottom line is that we have a technology available today that can provide abundant green energy for the foreseeable future with no shortage of the fuel little to no greenhouse gas emissions and manageable radioactive waste there's a huge upside and the drawbacks are few in my opinion this is good for mankind and we should at least consider it if not embrace it I'd like to thank Copenhagen atomics for their generosity and letting me tour their facilities to learn firsthand how molten salt reactors work I'll have a dedicated video about Copenhagen atomics including interviews with their leaders soon now as you might imagine there's a lot more details that I could have gotten into but I hope this video provides a good summary for you what do you think should we Embrace nuclear or not let me know in the comments I'll see you in the next video my [Music] friend

because its path information is known since the photon could only have come from the top slit similarly the entangled pair from the bottom slit hits a set of beam Splitters which randomly directs the photon to either detector 3 detector 4 or detector 5. if the photon goes to detector 3 it will also show a spread out pattern because its path information is known it could only have come from the bottom slit what makes things interesting is what happens to the photons that don't end up at two or three but end up a detector four or five these photons are fed into a set of optical instruments that effectively merge the beam from the right and left slit into a single recombined beam this is the Eraser part of the experiment it's called eraser because the photons path or Which Way information in those two cases is erased hence the patterns that you will see at four and five are always going to be interference patterns this is similar to the pattern made in the double slit experiment without a measuring device the two interference patterns from four and five look somewhat like this now you might ask where does the confusion of retro causality come in well the confusion is from the way this experiment is presented it's presented as a D1 changing its pattern to match the interference pattern at D4 or D5 when the photons end up there but showing a different pattern a spread out pattern if the photons end up a D2 or D3 so this implies that what happens at D2 D3 D4 or D5 influences what happens at D1 but since the path to D1 is shorter than the path to any of the other detectors the photons reach D1 before they reach D2 D3 D4 D5 so the implication is that the pattern of D1 which would be in the past is being affected by what happens in the future a D2 D3 D4 or D5 so people have naturally been led to think that the interference pattern of the Eraser will change the result on the original D1 screen this is wrong if we were to turn on the experiment and show the content of all five screens that is the screen from D1 the two screens showing photons from the individual slits detoured and D3 and the two screens D4 and D5 each showing half of the recombined beam that we would see that the patterns look something like this the quantum eraser has no effect on the original screen at D1 what's really happening is that the changing patterns are due to the scientists who are conducting this experiment selecting subsets of the photons in D1 to show the same pattern as that of each of the other detectors how is this done recall the first thing that happens after the double slit is the creation of entangled photons this means that there is a connection between the particles hitting the screen at D1 and the particles going down to the other detectors where we either measured them coming from separate slits causing no interference or as the recombined beam where we see an interference pattern since the path information of all the photons hitting D1 is known it is possible to single out or manually identify all individual photons ending up at any of the other detectors in other words using entanglement we can recover which entangled partner photons from any of the other four detectors hit the screen to D1 so for example if we manually sort out the entangled partner photons of the photons hitting D4 or D5 we can see the same exact interference pattern hidden within the spread out pattern of D1 but we see this only because we have now chosen a subsample from all the photons at D1 which of course is going to be interfering because they are entangled partners of the corresponding photons at D4 and D5 the same thing applies to D2 and D3 so in the presentations that you see including the one I originally made the interference pattern you see at D1 is nothing but a hand selected subset of the actual original spread out pattern at D1 corresponding to photons that ended up at D4 or D5 in fact if you combine the interference patterns at D4 and D5 you get a spread out pattern with no interference so what the quantum eraser is doing is creating two sub samples of the full non-interference picture which individually show interference but no interference when combined now here's The Clincher if you combine all the patterns from D2 D3 D4 and D5 you get precisely the same pattern that you have at D1 this should make sense because every entangled pair that ends up in D1 has a corresponding pair that ends up at one of the other four detectors so really you're not changing the past what you're really doing is in the future you are choosing a sub sample of the data you made in the past so I hope you find comfort in knowing that no one in the future can mess up your present they can only mess up your future what do you think leave your comment and I'll see you in the next video my friend [Music]
